. It suffices to prove the proposition under the assumption that
\langle x\rangle
is fully
\mathcal{F}
-centralized, and we will assume that this is the case. So we have
C_{S}(x)\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(C_{G}(x))
and
C_{S}(x)\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(x))
. Also,
\mathcal{F}_{C_{S}(x)}(C_{G}(x))=C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle x\rangle)=\mathcal{F}_{C_{S}(x)}(C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(x))
.

Clearly,
x
is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
t_{i}
. Lemma
6.9
(ii) implies that
C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle x\rangle)
has a unique component
\mathcal{E}
, and that
\mathcal{E}
is isomorphic to the
2
-fusion system of
SL_{n-1}(q)
. Applying Lemma
5.10
(i), we may conclude that
C_{G}(x)
has a unique
2
-component
J
with
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{F}_{C_{S}(x)\cap J}(J)
. By Lemma
5.10
(ii),
J/O(J)\cong SL_{n-1}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})/O(SL_{n-1}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0}))
for some nontrivial odd prime power
q_{0}
and some
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+,-\}
with
\varepsilon_{0}q_{0}\sim q
.

Set
Y:=C_{S}(x)\cap J
. By Lemma
6.9
(ii),
Y\trianglelefteq C_{S}(x)
and
C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle x\rangle)/Y
is nilpotent. Applying Corollary
2.18
, we may conclude that
J
is the only
2
-component of
C_{G}(x)
. Using Lemma
6.9
(ii), we see that any elementary abelian subgroup of
Y
of rank at least
2
lies in
\mathcal{S}_{n-1}
. As
Y\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(J)
, it follows that any elementary abelian
2
-subgroup of
J
of rank at least
2
is
G
-conjugate to a subgroup of
S
lying in
\mathcal{S}_{n-1}
.

It remains to show that
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon
and
q_{0}=q^{*}
. Define
s:=t_{i}
if
i=1
and
s:=t_{A}
, where
A:=\{1,\dots,n-1\}
, if
i=n-1
. Then we have
s\in C_{G}(t)
, and
s
is
G
-conjugate to
x
. Set
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C_{G}(t)/O(C_{G}(t))
. Lemma
6.2
shows that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{s}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
. Hence,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
is a component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{s})
. As a consequence of Corollary
2.2
and Proposition
2.4
,
C_{G}(t)\cap C_{G}(s)
has a
2
-component
H
with
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{H}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
. Set
C:=C_{G}(s)
and
\widehat{C}:=C/O(C)
. Then
\widehat{H}
is a
2
-component of
C_{\widehat{C}}(\widehat{t})
. Since
s
is
G
-conjugate to
x
,
\widehat{C}
has precisely one component
J^{+}
, and
J^{+}
is isomorphic to
SL_{n-1}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})/O(SL_{n-1}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0}))
. By Lemma
2.5
,
\widehat{H}
is a
2
-component of
C_{J^{+}}(\widehat{t})
. We see from Corollary
3.46
(i) that
\widehat{H}
is in fact a component of
C_{J^{+}}(\widehat{t})
. It is easy to see that
\widehat{H}/Z(\widehat{H})\cong H/Z^{*}(H)\cong\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}/Z(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})\cong PSL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
. Note that
\varepsilon_{0}q_{0}\sim q\sim\varepsilon q^{*}
and in particular
({q_{0}}^{2}-1)_{2}=({q^{*}}^{2}-1)_{2}
. Using this, we may deduce from Corollary
3.46
(iii) that
q_{0}=q^{*}
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon
.
∎

6.3.
2
-components of centralizers of involutions conjugate to
w

Recall that we assume
\rho
to be an element of
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{*}
with order
(n,q-1)
. Recall moreover that if
n
is even and
\rho
is a non-square element of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, then
\widetilde{w}
denotes the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}&I_{n/2}\\
\rho I_{n/2}&\end{pmatrix}

and, if
\widetilde{w}\in SL_{n}(q)
, then
w
denotes its image in
PSL_{n}(q)
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Suppose that
w
is defined. Let
P
be a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(w)
, and let
\mathcal{F}
denote the fusion system
\mathcal{F}_{P}(C_{PSL_{n}(q)})(w))
. Then
\mathcal{F}
has precisely one component. This component is isomorphic to the
2
-fusion system of a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}(q^{2})
. If
Y
is its Sylow subgroup, then we have
Y\trianglelefteq P
, and
\mathcal{F}/Y
is nilpotent.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.6
(i),
C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(w)
has precisely one
2
-component
J
, and
J
is isomorphic to a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}(q^{2})
. Applying Proposition
2.16
and Lemma
3.21
, we may conclude that
\mathcal{F}_{P\cap J}(J)
is the only component of
\mathcal{F}
. The last statement of the lemma is given by Lemma
3.6
(ii).
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

Suppose that
w
is defined. Let
x
be an involution of
S
which is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
w
. Then
C_{G}(x)
has precisely one
2
-component, say
J
. The group
J/O(J)
is isomorphic to a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})
for some nontrivial odd prime power
q_{0}
and some
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+,-\}
with
q^{2}\sim\varepsilon_{0}q_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)=\mathcal{F}_{S}(PSL_{n}(q))
. It suffices to prove the proposition under the assumption that
\langle x\rangle
is fully
\mathcal{F}
-centralized, and we will assume that this is the case. So we have
C_{S}(x)\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(C_{G}(x))
and
C_{S}(x)\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(x))
. Also,
\mathcal{F}_{C_{S}(x)}(C_{G}(x))=C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle x\rangle)=\mathcal{F}_{C_{S}(x)}(C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(x))
.

As
x
is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
w
, Lemma
6.12
implies that
C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle x\rangle)
has precisely one component, say
\mathcal{E}
, and that
\mathcal{E}
is isomorphic to the
2
-fusion system of a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}(q^{2})
. By Lemma
5.10
(i),
C_{G}(x)
has a unique
2
-component
J
such that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{F}_{C_{S}(x)\cap J}(J)
. Set
Y:=C_{S}(x)\cap J
. As a consequence of Lemma
6.12
, we have
Y\trianglelefteq C_{S}(x)
, and the factor system
C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle x\rangle)/Y
is nilpotent. So, by Corollary
2.18
,
J
is the only
2
-component of
C_{G}(x)
. Lemma
5.10
(iii) shows that
J/O(J)
is isomorphic to a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})
for some nontrivial odd prime power
q_{0}
and some
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+,-\}
with
q^{2}\sim\varepsilon_{0}q_{0}
.
∎

7.
The components of
C_{G}(t)

The goal of this section is to determine the isomorphism types of
K
and
L
. In order to do so, we will apply the signalizer functor techniques introduced by Gorenstein and Walter in
[
32
]
. In particular, we will see that
L
is isomorphic to
SL_{2}(q^{*})
. This will enable us in Section
8
to prove that a certain collection of conjugates of
L
generates a subgroup
G_{0}
of
G
which is isomorphic to a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{n}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
and normal in
G
. This will complete the proof of Theorem
5.2
.

7.1.
3
-generation of involution centralizers

For each
3\leq i\leq n
, we define
\mathcal{U}_{i}
to be the set of all subgroups
U
of
PSL_{n}(q)
such that
U
has a subgroup
E
with
E\in\mathcal{S}_{i}
and
m(E)\geq 3
. The following lemma will be important later in this section.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
1\leq i<n
such that
t_{i}
is defined. Suppose that
i\leq\frac{n}{2}
if
n
is even. Let
x
be an involution of
S
such that
x
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{i}
and such that
\langle x\rangle
is fully
\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)
-centralized. Then
C_{G}(x)
is
3
-generated in the sense of Definition
3.35
. Moreover, if
i\geq 4
, then we have

C_{G}(x)=\langle N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)\ |\ U\leq C_{S}(x),U\in\mathcal{U}_{i}\rangle.

If
i=2
, then we have

C_{G}(x)=\langle N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)\ |\ U\leq C_{S}(x),U\in\mathcal{U}_{n-2}\rangle.

Proof.

Set
C:=C_{G}(x)
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. Recall that
L_{2^{\prime}}(C)
denotes the subgroup of
C
generated by the
2
-components of
C
and that
L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})
denotes the product of all components of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
. Clearly,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}=L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})
.

First we consider the case
(n,i)\neq(6,3)
. Then, by Propositions
6.1
,
6.10
and
6.11
,
C
has a
2
-component
J
such that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}\cong SL_{k}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})/O(SL_{k}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*}))
for some
k\geq 4
and such that any elementary abelian subgroup of
Y:=C_{S}(x)\cap J
of rank at least
2
lies in
\mathcal{S}_{k}
. If
i\geq 4
, then we may assume that
k=i
, and if
i=2
, then
k=n-2
.

Clearly,
Y\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(J)
. By Lemma
3.37
, we have that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
is
3
-generated. So we have

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq Y,m(U)\geq 3\rangle.

Set
X:=C_{S}(x)\cap L_{2^{\prime}}(C)
. By the Frattini argument,
L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}N_{L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y})
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}=L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{X})
. It follows that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ \textnormal{$U=X$, or $U\leq Y$ and $m(U)\geq 3$}\rangle.

Lemma
2.1
implies that
C
is generated by
O(C)
together with the normalizers
N_{C}(U)
, where
U=X
, or
U\leq Y
and
m(U)\geq 3
.

Let
E
denote the subgroup of
S
generated by
t
,
t_{\{n-2,n-1\}}
,
t_{\{n-3,n-2\}}
and
t_{\{n-4,n-3\}}
. Clearly,
E\cong E_{16}
. Since
x
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{i}
and
E\leq C_{G}(t_{i})
, there is a subgroup
E_{x}
of
C_{S}(x)
which is
G
-conjugate to
E
. By
[
28
, Proposition 11.23]
, we have

O(C)=\langle C_{O(C)}(D)\ |\ D\leq E_{x},D\cong E_{8}\rangle.

As remarked above, any elementary abelian subgroup of
Y
of rank at least
2
lies in
\mathcal{S}_{k}
. So, if
U\leq Y
and
m(U)\geq 3
, then
U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
. Also
X\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
. Clearly, any
E_{8}
-subgroup of
E_{x}
lies in
\mathcal{S}_{k}
and hence in
\mathcal{U}_{k}
. We therefore have

C=\langle N_{C}(U)\ |\ U\leq C_{S}(x),U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\rangle.

Consequently,
C
is
3
-generated, and the last two statements of the lemma are satisfied.

Suppose now that
(n,i)=(6,3)
. By Proposition
6.10
,
C
has precisely two
2
-components
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
, and we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}\cong PSL_{3}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})\cong\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
. Set
Y_{1}:=C_{S}(x)\cap J_{1}
and
Y_{2}:=C_{S}(x)\cap J_{2}
. Since
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
is
2
-generated by Lemma
3.36
, we have

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq Y_{1},m(U)\geq 2\rangle.

Let
y
be an involution of
Y_{2}
. We have
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}]=1
by
[
37
, 6.5.3]
, and so
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
. As
Z(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J})=1
, we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}\not\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
. Now let
U\leq Y_{1}
with
m(U)\geq 2
. Then
\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}\rangle
has rank at least
3
. Moreover, it is clear that
N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})
normalizes
\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}\rangle
. Thus

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq Y_{1}Y_{2},m(U)\geq 3\rangle.

Interchanging the roles of
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
, we also see that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq Y_{1}Y_{2},m(U)\geq 3\rangle.

By the Frattini argument,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y})
. Lemma
2.1
implies that
C
is generated by
O(C)
together with the normalizers
N_{C}(U)
, where
U\leq Y_{1}Y_{2}
and
m(U)\geq 3
. For any
E_{16}
-subgroup
A
of
C_{S}(x)
, we have

O(C)=\langle C_{O(C)}(B)\ |\ B\leq A,B\cong E_{8}\rangle.

by
[
28
, Proposition 11.23]
. It follows that
C
is
3
-generated. The proof is now complete.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Suppose that
w
is defined. Let
x
be an involution of
S
which is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
w
. Then
C_{G}(x)
is
3
-generated.

Proof.

Set
C:=C_{G}(x)
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. By Proposition
6.13
,
C
has a unique
2
-component
J
, and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
is isomorphic to a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})
for some nontrivial odd prime power
q_{0}
and some
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+,-\}
with
q^{2}\sim\varepsilon_{0}q_{0}
. Note that
q_{0}\equiv\varepsilon_{0}\mod 8
.

First we prove that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
is
3
-generated. Let
R
be a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
C
and
Y:=R\cap J
. We consider two cases.

Case 1:
n\geq 8
.

By Lemma
3.37
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
is
3
-generated. Hence

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq Y,m(U)\geq 3\rangle.

By the Frattini argument,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y})
. So
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
is
3
-generated.

Case 2:
n=6
.

We have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}\cong PSL_{3}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})
. By Lemma
3.36
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
is
2
-generated. Applying the Frattini argument, we may conclude that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq Y,m(U)\geq 2\rangle.

Now let
U\leq Y
with
m(U)\geq 2
. Since
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}
is a central involution of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
and
Z(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J})
is trivial, we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}\not\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{J}
and hence
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}\not\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U}
. It follows
\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}\rangle
has rank at least
3
. Moreover, as
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}
is central in
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
, we have
N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\leq N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}\rangle)
. Clearly,
\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{x}\rangle\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{R}
. It follows that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}=\langle N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})\ |\ U\leq R,m(U)\geq 3\rangle.

Hence
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
is
3
-generated.

Applying Lemma
2.1
, we may conclude that
C
is generated by
O(C)
together with the normalizers
N_{C}(U)
, where
U\leq R
and
m(U)\geq 3
. By Lemma
3.6
(iii),
R
has an elementary abelian
2
-subgroup of rank
4
, say
A
. By
[
28
, Proposition 11.23]
, we have

O(C)=\langle C_{O(C)}(B)\ |\ B\leq A,B\cong E_{8}\rangle.

So
C
is
3
-generated.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
x
be an involution of
S
. Then
C_{G}(x)
is
3
-generated.

Proof.

As a consequence of Proposition
3.5
,
x
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{i}
for some
1\leq i<n
such that
t_{i}
is defined or
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
w
(if defined). So the statement follows from Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
.
∎

7.2.
The case
q^{*}=3

Recall that our goal is to determine the isomorphism types of
K
and
L
. First we will deal with the case
q^{*}=3
. We will prove that, in this case,
O(C_{G}(t))=1
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
x
be an involution of
S
, and let
J
be a
2
-component of
C_{G}(x)
. Let
1\leq i<n
such that
t_{i}
is defined. Suppose that
q^{*}=3
and that
x
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{i}
. Then
J/O(J)
is locally balanced.

Proof.

By Propositions
6.8
(iii),
6.10
and
6.11
, we have
J/O(J)\cong SL_{k}^{\varepsilon}(3)
for some
3\leq k<n
. So
J/O(J)
is locally balanced by Lemma
3.47
.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
P
and
Q
be subgroups of
S
.

(i)

If
P\in\mathcal{S}
and
m(P)\leq 2
, then there is a subgroup
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}
of
S
such that
P<\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}\in\mathcal{S}
and
m(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})=3
.

(ii)

If
P
and
Q
are elements of
\mathcal{S}
of rank at least
3
, then there exist some
m\geq 1
and a sequence

P=P_{1},\dots,P_{m}=Q,

where
P_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq m
, is a subgroup of
S
of rank at least
2
lying in
\mathcal{S}
and where

P_{i}\subseteq P_{i+1}\ \textnormal{or}\ P_{i+1}\subseteq P_{i}

for all
1\leq i<m
.

Proof.

Suppose that
P\in\mathcal{S}
and
m(P)\leq 2
. Let
\widetilde{S}
be a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
SL_{n}(q)
such that
S
is the image of
\widetilde{S}
in
PSL_{n}(q)
. Note that
\widetilde{S}
is unique. Since
P
is an element of
\mathcal{S}
, there exists some elementary abelian
2
-subgroup
\widetilde{P}
of
SL_{n}(q)
such that
P
is the image of
\widetilde{P}
in
PSL_{n}(q)
. Clearly,
\widetilde{P}\leq\widetilde{S}
. We have
m(\widetilde{P})\leq 3
as
m(P)\leq 2
. By Corollary
3.34
,
\widetilde{P}
is contained in an
E_{16}
-subgroup of
\widetilde{S}
. This implies (i).

We now prove (ii). Suppose that
P
and
Q
are elements of
\mathcal{S}
of rank at least
3
. There are elementary abelian subgroups
\widetilde{P}
and
\widetilde{Q}
of
SL_{n}(q)
such that
P
is the image of
\widetilde{P}
in
PSL_{n}(q)
and such that
Q
is the image of
\widetilde{Q}
in
PSL_{n}(q)
. Clearly,
\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q}\leq\widetilde{S}
. Also
m(\widetilde{P}),m(\widetilde{Q})\geq 3
. Since
\widetilde{S}
is
3
-connected by Corollary
3.33
, there exist some
m\geq 1
and a sequence

\widetilde{P}=\widetilde{P}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{P}_{n}=\widetilde{Q},

where
\widetilde{P}_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq m
) is an elementary abelian subgroup of
\widetilde{S}
of rank at least
3
and where

\widetilde{P}_{i}\subseteq\widetilde{P}_{i+1}\ \textnormal{or}\ \widetilde{P}_{i+1}\subseteq\widetilde{P}_{i}

for all
1\leq i<m
. Let
P_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq m
, denote the image of
\widetilde{P}_{i}
in
S
. Then the sequence

P=P_{1},\dots,P_{m}=Q

has the desired properties.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Suppose that
q^{*}=3
. For each elementary abelian subgroup
E
of
S
of rank at least
2
, let

W_{E}:=\langle O(C_{G}(x))\ |\ x\in E^{\#}\rangle.

Let
P
and
Q
be subgroups of
S
with
P,Q\in\mathcal{S}
and
m(P),m(Q)\geq 3
. Then
W_{P}=W_{Q}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.5
(ii), there exist some
m\geq 1
and a sequence

P=P_{1},\dots,P_{m}=Q,

where
P_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq m
, is a subgroup of
S
of rank at least
2
lying in
\mathcal{S}
and where

P_{i}\subseteq P_{i+1}\ \textnormal{or}\ P_{i+1}\subseteq P_{i}

for all
1\leq i<m
. By Lemma
7.5
(i), there is a subgroup
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}
of
S
such that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}\in\mathcal{S}
,
m(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})\geq 3
and
P_{i}\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}
for each
1\leq i\leq m
.

Let
1\leq i\leq m
and let
x
be an involution of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}
. Also let
J
be a
2
-component of
C_{G}(x)
. As
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}\in\mathcal{S}
, we have that
x
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{j}
for some even
2\leq j<n
. Therefore, by Lemma
7.4
,
J/O(J)
is locally balanced. Applying
[
32
, Corollary 5.6]
, we may conclude that
G
is balanced with respect to
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}
.

Let
1\leq i<m
. We have
m(P_{i}\cap P_{i+1})\geq 2
since
P_{i}\subseteq P_{i+1}
or
P_{i+1}\subseteq P_{i}
and
m(P_{i}),m(P_{i+1})\geq 2
. Hence
m(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}\cap\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})\geq 2
. Proposition
2.8
(ii) implies

W_{P_{i}}=W_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}=W_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}\cap\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}=W_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}=W_{P_{i+1}}.

Consequently,
W_{P}=W_{Q}
, as wanted.
∎

Proposition 7.7
.

Suppose that
q^{*}=3
. Let
x
be an involution of
S
which is
G
-conjugate to
t_{i}
for some even
2\leq i<n
. Then we have
O(C_{G}(x))=1
. In particular,
O(C_{G}(t))=1
.

Proof.

We follow the pattern of the proof of
[
32
, Theorem 9.1]
. Let
E
be the subgroup of
S
consisting of all
t_{A}
, where
A\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
has even order. For each elementary abelian
2
-subgroup
A
of
G
of rank at least
2
, let

W_{A}:=\langle O(C_{G}(y))\ |\ y\in A^{\#}\rangle.

Set
W_{0}:=W_{E}
and
M:=N_{G}(W_{0})
. We accomplish the proof step by step.

(1)
N_{G}(S)\leq M
.

Let
g\in N_{G}(S)
. Clearly,
E\in\mathcal{S}
, and it is easy to note
E^{g}
still lies in
\mathcal{S}
. Lemma
7.6
implies that
W_{0}=W_{E^{g}}
. On the other hand, we have
(W_{0})^{g}=W_{E^{g}}
by Proposition
2.8
(i). So we have
(W_{0})^{g}=W_{0}
and hence
g\in M
.

(2)
Let
y
be an involution of
S
such that
y
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{j}
for some even
2\leq j<n
. Then
y
is
M
-conjugate to
t_{j}
.

We have
\langle y\rangle\in\mathcal{S}
. By Lemma
7.5
(i), there is a subgroup
A
of
S
with
\langle y\rangle\leq A
,
A\in\mathcal{S}
and
m(A)=3
. As a consequence of Lemma
3.22
, there is an element
g
of
G
with
A^{g}\leq E
. By Lemma
7.6
and Proposition
2.8
(i), we have
(W_{0})^{g}=(W_{A})^{g}=W_{A^{g}}=W_{0}
. Thus
g\in M
.

We have
y^{g}\in E
, and
y^{g}
is
G
-conjugate and hence
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
t_{j}
. It is rather easy to show that an element of
E
is
N_{PSL_{n}(q)}(E)
-conjugate to
t_{j}
if it is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
t_{j}
. So
y^{g}
is
N_{PSL_{n}(q)}(E)
-conjugate and hence
N_{G}(E)
-conjugate to
t_{j}
. As
N_{G}(E)\leq M
, it follows that
y^{g}
is
M
-conjugate to
t_{j}
. Hence
y
is
M
-conjugate to
t_{j}
.

(3)
Let
y
be an involution of
S
such that
y
is
G
-conjugate to
t_{j}
for some even
2\leq j<n
. Then
C_{G}(y)\leq M
.

Because of (2), we may assume that
\langle y\rangle
is fully
\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)
-centralized. Then, by Lemma
7.1
,
C_{G}(y)
is generated by the normalizers
N_{C_{G}(y)}(U)
, where
U
is a subgroup of
C_{S}(y)
such that there exists
B\leq U
with
B\in\mathcal{S}
and
m(B)\geq 3
. It suffices to show that each such normalizer lies in
M
.

Let
U
and
B
be as above and let
g\in N_{C_{G}(y)}(U)
. By Lemma
7.6
and Proposition
2.8
(i), we have
(W_{0})^{g}=(W_{B})^{g}=W_{B^{g}}=W_{0}
. Thus
g\in M
and hence
N_{C_{G}(y)}(U)\leq M
, as required.

(4)
Let
y
be an involution of
S
. Then
C_{G}(y)\leq M
.

We can see from Lemmas
3.14
and
3.15
that
Z(S)
has an involution
s
which is
G
-conjugate to
t_{j}
for some even
2\leq j<n
. Let
P
be a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
C_{G}(y)
with
s\in P
. By (1),
s\in M
and hence
s\in P\cap M
. Now let
r\in N_{P}(P\cap M)
. Then
s^{r}\in P\cap M
. As a consequence of (1) and (2),
s^{r}
and
s
are
M
-conjugate to
t_{j}
. Therefore, there is some
m\in M
with
s^{r}=s^{m}
. We have
rm^{-1}\in C_{G}(s)
, and so
rm^{-1}\in M
by (3). Hence
r\in M
. Consequently,
N_{P}(P\cap M)=P\cap M
. It follows that
P=P\cap M
.

Let
U\leq P
with
m(U)\geq 3
and let
g\in N_{C_{G}(y)}(U)
. By Lemma
2.3
, any
E_{8}
-subgroup of
S
has an involution which is the image of an involution of
SL_{n}(q)
. Since
m(U)\geq 3
, it follows that
U
has an element
u
which is
G
-conjugate to
t_{k}
for some even
2\leq k<n
. By the preceding paragraph,
u,u^{g}\in U\leq P\leq M
. As a consequence of (1) and (2),
u
and
u^{g}
are
M
-conjugate to
t_{k}
. So there is some
m\in M
with
u^{g}=u^{m}
. Hence
gm^{-1}\in C_{G}(u)
. From (3), we see that
C_{G}(u)\leq M
, and so
gm^{-1}\in M
. Thus
g\in M
and hence
N_{C_{G}(y)}(U)\leq M
. Since
C_{G}(y)
is
3
-generated by Corollary
7.3
, it follows that
C_{G}(y)\leq M
.

(5)
M=G.

Assume that
M\neq G
. By
[
28
, Proposition 17.11]
, we may deduce from (1) and (4) that
M
is strongly embedded in
G
, i.e.
M\cap M^{g}
has odd order for any
g\in G\setminus M
. Applying
[
49
, Chapter 6, 4.4]
, it follows that
G
has only one conjugacy class of involutions. On the other hand, we see from Proposition
3.5
that
G
has at least two conjugacy classes of involutions. This contradiction shows that
M=G
.

(6)
Conclusion.

Let
y\in E^{\#}
and let
J
be a
2
-component of
C_{G}(y)
. By Lemma
7.4
,
J/O(J)
is locally balanced. So, by
[
32
, Corollary 5.6]
,
G
is balanced with respect to
E
. Proposition
2.8
(ii) implies that
W_{0}
has odd order. By (5), we have
M=G
and hence
W_{0}\trianglelefteq G
. As
O(G)=1
by Hypothesis
5.1
, it follows that
W_{0}=1
. So we have
O(C_{G}(y))=1
for all
y\in E^{\#}
, and the statement of the proposition follows.
∎

Proposition
7.7
implies that if
q^{*}=3
, then
K\cong SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(3)
and
L\cong SL_{2}(3)
. Our next goal is to find the isomorphism types of
K
and
L
for the case
q^{*}\neq 3
.

In general,
O(C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(t))
is not trivial. So, if
q^{*}
is not assumed to be
3
, we have no chance to prove that
O(C_{G}(t))=1
. However, we will be able to show that

\Delta_{G}(F)=\bigcap_{a\in F^{\#}}O(C_{G}(a))=1

for any Klein four subgroup
F
of
G
consisting of elements of the form
t_{A}
, where
A\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
has even order. This will later enable us to determine the isomorphism types of
K
and
L
for the case
q^{*}\neq 3
.

7.3.
2
-balance of
G

In this subsection, we prove that
G
is
2
-balanced when
q^{*}\neq 3
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Set
C:=C_{G}(t)
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. Let
F
be a Klein four subgroup of
C
. Then
[\Delta_{\overline{C}}(\overline{F}),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}]=1
.

Proof.

We closely follow arguments found in the proof of
[
32
, Theorem 5.2]
.

First we consider the case that
F
has a nontrivial element
y
such that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
. Then
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
normalizes
O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
and, as
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}\trianglelefteq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
,
O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
also normalizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
. It follows that

[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))]\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}\cap O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})).

Hence,
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))]
is a subgroup of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
with odd order. By
[
37
, 1.5.5]
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
normalizes
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))]
. It follows that

[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))]\leq O(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}).

As
O(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})=1
, this implies that
O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
. By definition of
\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})
, we have
\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})\leq O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
. Consequently,
\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})
centralizes
\overline{K}
.

Now we treat the case that
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})=1
. For each subgroup or element
X
of
C
, let
\widehat{X}
denote the image of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{X}
in
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}/C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
. Since
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})=1
, we have
\widehat{F}\cong\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}
, and so
\widehat{F}
is a Klein four subgroup of
\widehat{C}
. As
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}\cong SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})/O(SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*}))
, we have that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
is locally
2
-balanced (see Lemma
3.48
). Using this together with the fact that the group
\widehat{C}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}/C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
is isomorphic to a subgroup of
\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
containing
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
, we may conclude that
\Delta_{\widehat{C}}(\widehat{F})=1
. By
[
32
, Proposition 3.11]
, if
X
is a finite group,
B
a
2
-subgroup of
X
and
N\trianglelefteq X
, then the image of
O(C_{X}(B))
in
X/N
lies in
O(C_{X/N}(BN/N))
. Thus, if
y
is an involution of
F
, then the image of
O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
in
\widehat{C}
lies in
O(C_{\widehat{C}}(\widehat{y}))
. It follows that the image of
\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})
in
\widehat{C}
is contained in
\Delta_{\widehat{C}}(\widehat{F})=1
. Hence
\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
C:=C_{G}(t)
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. Then
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})\cap C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
is a
2
-group.

Proof.

For convenience, we denote
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})\cap C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
by
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
. Since
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
is core-free, we have that
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
is core-free. So it is enough to prove that
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
is
2
-nilpotent. By
[
39
, Theorem 1.4]
, it suffices to show that
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
has a nilpotent
2
-fusion system.

Let
X
denote the subgroup of
T
consisting of all elements of
T
of the form

\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&B\end{pmatrix}Z(SL_{n}(q))

with
A\in W\cap Z(GL_{n-2}(q))
,
B\in V\cap Z(GL_{2}(q))
and
\mathrm{det}(A)\mathrm{det}(B)=1
.

Let
A\in W
and
B\in V
with
\mathrm{det}(A)\mathrm{det}(B)=1
and

m:=\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&B\end{pmatrix}Z(SL_{n}(q))\in T.

Assume that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
. Then we have
A\in Z(GL_{n-2}(q))
by Lemma
6.2
. Since
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}
also centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{X}
. Thus
m
centralizes
X_{2}
, and so
B
centralizes
V\cap SL_{2}(q)
. Lemma
3.17
implies that
B\in Z(GL_{2}(q))
. So we have
m\in X
. Conversely, if
A\in Z(GL_{n-2}(q))
and
B\in Z(GL_{2}(q))
, then
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}\in C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
as a consequence of Lemmas
6.2
and
3.43
. It follows that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{T}\cap C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{X}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}_{S}(PSL_{n}(q))=\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)
. Since
X
is central in
C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(t)
, the only subsystem of
C_{\mathcal{F}}(\langle t\rangle)
on
X
is the nilpotent fusion system on
X
. It follows that
\mathcal{F}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{X}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}))
is nilpotent. So
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
has a nilpotent
2
-fusion system, as required.
∎

In the following lemma,
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
have the meanings given to them after Lemma
6.5
.

Lemma 7.10
.

Set
C:=C_{G}(t)
. Suppose that
q^{*}\neq 3
. Then
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
and
L
are the only
2
-components of
C_{C}(u)
. Moreover, the following hold:

(i)

A_{1}
is the only
2
-component of
C_{C}(u)
containing
u
.

(ii)

A_{2}
is the only
2
-component of
C_{C}(u)
containing neither
u
nor
t
.

(iii)

L
is the only
2
-component of
C_{C}(u)
containing
t
.

Proof.

By definition,
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are
2
-components of
C_{C}(u)
. Also, it is clear from the definition of
L
(see Proposition
6.8
) that
L
is a
2
-component of
C_{C}(u)
.

Set
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. As a consequence of Lemma
6.4
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
are the only
2
-components of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
. Moreover,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
is a component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
. So Lemma
2.5
shows that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
are the only
2
-components of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
. As we have observed after Lemma
6.5
, there is a bijection from the set of
2
-components of
C_{C}(u)
to the set of
2
-components of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
sending each
2
-component
A
of
C_{C}(u)
to
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
. Therefore,
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
and
L
are the only
2
-components of
C_{C}(u)
.

It remains to prove (i), (ii) and (iii). We have
T_{1}\leq A_{1}
by Lemma
6.6
and thus
u\in A_{1}
. From the definition of
L
, it is clear that
t\in L
. Moreover,
u\not\in L
since
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t}
is the only involution of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
. Similarly,
t\not\in A_{1}
. Also, it is easy to see from Lemma
6.4
that
u
and
t
cannot be elements of
A_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 7.11
.

Suppose that
q^{*}\neq 3
. Let
F
be a Klein four subgroup of
T
. Then we have
\Delta_{G}(F)\cap C_{G}(t)\leq O(C_{G}(t))
.

Proof.

Set
C:=C_{G}(t)
,
D:=\Delta_{G}(F)\cap C
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. We are going to show that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
is trivial.

A direct calculation shows that
D\leq\Delta_{C}(F)
. For each
a\in F^{\#}
, we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{O}\leq O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{a}))
as a consequence of Corollary
2.2
. Therefore, we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}\leq\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})
, and hence
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})
. Lemma
7.8
implies that
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}]=1
. In particular,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
. Fix a subgroup
D_{0}
of
C_{C}(u)
with
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
. Also, let
g\in G
with
u^{g}=t
and
t^{g}=u
(such an element exists by Lemma
6.7
). Note that
(D_{0})^{g}\leq(C_{C}(u))^{g}=C_{C}(u)
.

We accomplish the proof step by step.

(1)
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
and
L
are normal subgroups of
C_{C}(u)
.

This is immediate from Lemma
7.10
.

(2)
There is a group isomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
which maps
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
to
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
and
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
to
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
.

Let
\mathrm{Aut}_{D_{0}}(L/O(L))
denote the image of
\mathrm{Aut}_{D_{0}}(L)
under the natural group homomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(L)\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(L/O(L))
. Also, let
\mathrm{Aut}_{(D_{0})^{g}}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))
denote the image of
\mathrm{Aut}_{(D_{0})^{g}}(A_{1})
under the natural group homomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(A_{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))
.

From Lemma
7.10
, it is clear that
(A_{1})^{g^{-1}}=L
. The group isomorphism
c_{g^{-1}}|_{A_{1},L}
induces a group isomorphism
A_{1}/O(A_{1})\rightarrow L/O(L)
, and this group isomorphism induces a group isomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(L/O(L))
. By a direct calculation, the group isomorphism just mentioned maps
\mathrm{Aut}_{(D_{0})^{g}}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))
to
\mathrm{Aut}_{D_{0}}(L/O(L))
and
\mathrm{Inn}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))
to
\mathrm{Inn}(L/O(L))
.

We have
A_{1}/(A_{1}\cap O(C))\cong\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\cong SL_{2}(q^{*})
. As
SL_{2}(q^{*})
is core-free, it follows that
A_{1}\cap O(C)=O(A_{1})
. So the natural group homomorphism
A_{1}\rightarrow\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
induces a group isomorphism
A_{1}/O(A_{1})\rightarrow\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
. This group isomorphism induces a group isomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
. By a direct calculation, the group isomorphism just mentioned maps
\mathrm{Aut}_{(D_{0})^{g}}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))
to
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
and
\mathrm{Inn}(A_{1}/O(A_{1}))
to
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
. In a very similar way, we obtain an isomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(L/O(L))\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
which maps
\mathrm{Aut}_{D_{0}}(L/O(L))
to
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})=\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
and
\mathrm{Inn}(L/O(L))
to
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
.

As a consequence of the preceding observations, there is a group isomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
which maps
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
to
\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
and
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
to
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
, as asserted.

(3)
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})\leq\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
.

As observed above,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}
. In particular,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
. This implies that
[D_{0},A_{2}]\leq O(C)
. As
D_{0}
normalizes
A_{2}
by (1), we also have that
[D_{0},A_{2}]\leq A_{2}
. Consequently,
[D_{0},A_{2}]\leq O(A_{2})
. Because of Lemma
7.10
, we have
(A_{2})^{g}=A_{2}
. It follows that
[(D_{0})^{g},A_{2}]\leq O(A_{2})
. This easily implies
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}]\leq O(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
. As
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\cong SL_{n-4}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
by Lemma
6.4
, we have
O(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})\leq Z(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
. It follows that
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}]=[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}]\leq[Z(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}]=1
. The Three Subgroups Lemma
[
37
, 1.5.6]
implies
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}]=[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}]=1
. Hence,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
. By (1),
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
normalizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
. Clearly,
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
has odd order. The assertion now follows from Lemmas
6.4
(iii),
3.49
and
3.50
.

(4)
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq\bigcap_{y\in F^{\#}}O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
.

As a consequence of (2) and (3), we have
\mathrm{Aut}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})\leq\mathrm{Inn}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
. This implies
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
. By
[
37
, 6.5.3]
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
. As observed above,
[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}]=1
and hence
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
. It follows that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
is a subgroup of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})\cap C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}))
. By Lemma
7.9
,
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})\cap C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K})
is a
2
-group. As
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
has odd order and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}\trianglelefteq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
, this implies that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
. Now we see that

\displaystyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}

\displaystyle\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}\cap\Delta_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F})

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{y\in F^{\#}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}\cap O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})))

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{y\in F^{\#}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})\cap O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})))

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{y\in F^{\#}}O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})).

(5)
Conclusion.

As
F
is a Klein four subgroup of
T
, we have
F=\langle y_{1},y_{2}\rangle
for two commuting involutions
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
of
T
. For
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have

y_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{i}&\\
&B_{i}\end{pmatrix}Z(SL_{n}(q))

for some
A_{i}\in W
and
B_{i}\in V
with
\mathrm{det}(A_{i})\mathrm{det}(B_{i})=1
. Let
y_{3}:=y_{1}y_{2},A_{3}:=A_{1}A_{2}
and
B_{3}:=B_{1}B_{2}
. As
y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}
are involutions, we have
(B_{i})^{2}\in Z(GL_{2}(q))
for each
i\in\{1,2,3\}
.

It is easy to note that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{X}\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L})
. If
B\in V\cap SL_{2}(q)
and

y:=\begin{pmatrix}I_{n-2}&\\
&B\end{pmatrix}Z(SL_{n}(q))\in X_{2},

then

y^{y_{i}}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{n-2}&\\
&B^{B_{i}}\end{pmatrix}Z(SL_{n}(q))

for each
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Applying Lemma
3.51
, we deduce that

\bigcap_{y\in F^{\#}}O(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))=1.

So we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}=1
by (4). This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.12
.

Suppose that
q^{*}\neq 3
. Then
G
is
2
-balanced.

Proof.

Let
F
be a Klein four subgroup of
G
and let
a
be an involution of
G
centralizing
F
. We have to show that
\Delta_{G}(F)\cap C_{G}(a)\leq O(C_{G}(a))
.

Assume that
a
is
G
-conjugate to
t
. Then there is some
g\in G
with
a^{g}=t
and
F^{g}\leq T
. By Lemma
7.11
, we have
\Delta_{G}(F^{g})\cap C_{G}(t)\leq O(C_{G}(t))
. Clearly
\Delta_{G}(F)^{g}=\Delta_{G}(F^{g})
. It follows that
\Delta_{G}(F)\cap C_{G}(a)\leq O(C_{G}(a))
.

Assume now that
a
is not
G
-conjugate to
t
. Let
J
be a
2
-component of
C_{G}(a)
. By Propositions
6.10
,
6.11
and
6.13
, either
J/O(J)\cong SL_{k}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})/O(SL_{k}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*}))
for some
k\geq 3
, or
J/O(J)
is isomorphic to a nontrivial quotient of
SL_{\frac{n}{2}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}(q_{0})
for some nontrivial odd prime power
q_{0}
and some
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+,-\}
. So
J/O(J)
is locally
2
-balanced by Lemma
3.48
. Applying
[
32
, Theorem 5.2]
, we may conclude that
\Delta_{C_{G}(a)}(F)\leq O(C_{G}(a))
. A direct calculation shows that
\Delta_{G}(F)\cap C_{G}(a)\leq\Delta_{C_{G}(a)}(F)
. Hence
\Delta_{G}(F)\cap C_{G}(a)\leq O(C_{G}(a))
.
∎

7.4.
The case
q^{*}\neq 3
: Triviality of
\Delta_{G}(F)

Lemma 7.13
.

Suppose that
q^{*}\neq 3
. Assume moreover that
q\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ 4
or
n\geq 7
. Then we have
\Delta_{G}(F)=1
for each Klein four subgroup
F
of
S
.

Proof.

We follow the pattern of the proof of
[
32
, Theorem 9.1]
.

For each elementary abelian
2
-subgroup
A
of
G
of rank at least
3
, we define

W_{A}:=\langle\Delta_{G}(F)\ |\ F\leq A,m(F)=2\rangle.

Let
P
and
Q
be elementary abelian subgroups of
S
of rank at least
3
. We claim that
W_{P}=W_{Q}
. By Corollary
3.33
(iii),
S
is
3
-connected. So there exist a natural number
m\geq 1
and a sequence

P=P_{1},\dots,P_{m}=Q

such that
P_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq m
, is an elementary abelian subgroup of
S
of rank at least
3
and such that

P_{i}\subseteq P_{i+1}\ \textnormal{or}\ P_{i+1}\subseteq P_{i}

for all
1\leq i<m
. By Lemma
7.12
,
G
is
2
-balanced. Proposition
2.8
(ii) implies that
W_{P_{i}}=W_{P_{i+1}}
for all
1\leq i<m
. Therefore,
W_{P}=W_{Q}
, as asserted.

We use
W_{0}
to denote
W_{P}
, where
P
is an elementary abelian subgroup of
S
of rank at least
3
. Let
M:=N_{G}(W_{0})
. We accomplish the proof step by step.

(1)
N_{G}(S)\leq M
.

Let
g\in N_{G}(S)
. Take an elementary abelian subgroup
P
of
S
with
m(P)\geq 3
. By Proposition
2.8
(i), we have
(W_{0})^{g}=(W_{P})^{g}=W_{P^{g}}=W_{0}
. Thus
g\in M
.

(2)
Let
x
be an involution of
S
. Then
C_{G}(x)\leq M
.

By Corollary
3.34
, there is an elementary abelian subgroup
P
of
S
with
x\in P
and
m(P)=4
. Clearly,
P\leq C_{G}(x)
. Let
R
be a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
C_{G}(x)
containing
P
. By Corollary
7.3
,
C_{G}(x)
is
3
-generated. Hence,
C_{G}(x)
is generated by the normalizers
N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)
, where
U\leq R
and
m(U)\geq 3
. It suffices to show that each such normalizer lies in
M
.

So let
U
be a subgroup of
R
with
m(U)\geq 3
, and let
g\in N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)
. Let
Q
be an elementary abelian subgroup of
U
with
m(Q)=3
, and let
h\in G
with
R^{h}\leq S
. Then
W_{Q^{h}}=W_{Q^{gh}}=W_{P^{h}}=W_{0}
. Proposition
2.8
(i) implies that
W_{Q}=W_{Q^{g}}=W_{P}=W_{0}
. Applying Proposition
2.8
(i) again, it follows that
(W_{0})^{g}=(W_{Q})^{g}=W_{Q^{g}}=W_{0}
. Hence
g\in M
and thus
N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)\leq M
.

(3)
M=G
.

Assume that
M\neq G
. By
[
28
, Proposition 17.11]
, we may deduce from (1) and (2) that
M
is strongly embedded in
G
, i.e.
M\cap M^{g}
has odd order for any
g\in G\setminus M
. Applying
[
49
, Chapter 6, 4.4]
, it follows that
G
has only one conjugacy class of involutions. On the other hand, we see from Proposition
3.5
that
G
has at least two conjugacy classes of involutions. This contradiction shows that
M=G
.

(4)
Conclusion.

Let
F
be a Klein four subgroup of
S
. By Corollary
3.34
, there is an elementary abelian subgroup
P
of
S
with
F\leq P
and
m(P)=4
. Clearly,
\Delta_{G}(F)\leq W_{P}
. Since
G
is
2
-balanced,
W_{P}
has odd order by Proposition
2.8
(ii). Since
W_{P}=W_{0}
, we have
W_{P}\trianglelefteq G
by (3). As
O(G)=1
by Hypothesis
5.1
, it follows that
W_{P}=1
. Hence
\Delta_{G}(F)=1
.
∎

Next, we deal with the case that
n=6
,
q\equiv 3\mod 4
and
q^{*}\neq 3
. We show that, in this case,
\Delta_{G}(F)=1
for each Klein four subgroup
F
of
S
consisting of elements of the form
t_{A}
, where
A\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
has even order. We need the following lemma.

Lemma 7.14
.

Suppose that
q^{*}\neq 3
. Set
\ell:=n-4
. Let
E
be the subgroup of
T
consisting of all
t_{A}
, where
A\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
has even order. Let
E_{1}
denote the subgroup of
X_{1}
consisting of all
t_{A}
, where
A
is a subset of
\{1,\dots,n-2\}
of even order. Then we may choose elements
m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell}\in N_{K}(E_{1})
and an
E_{8}
-subgroup
E_{0}
of
E
with

K=\langle O(K),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0})),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))^{m_{1}},\dots,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))^{m_{\ell}}\rangle.

Proof.

Set
C:=C_{G}(t)
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. Let
H:=SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})/O(SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*}))
. Let
\widetilde{D}
be the subgroup of
SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
consisting of all diagonal matrices in
SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
with diagonal entries in
\{1,-1\}
, and let
D
denote the image of
\widetilde{D}
in
H
. Denote by
H_{1}
the image of

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&I_{n-4}\end{pmatrix}\ :\ A\in SL_{2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})\right\}

in
H
.

We claim that there is a group isomorphism
\psi:\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}\rightarrow H
which maps
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
to
D
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
to
H_{1}
. By Lemma
6.4
(iii), there is a group isomorphism
\varphi:\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}\rightarrow H
under which
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
corresponds to
H_{1}
. Since
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}
is the only involution of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
, we have that
{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}}^{\varphi}
is the image of
\mathrm{diag}(-1,-1,1,\dots,1)\in SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
in
H
. Clearly,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
is elementary abelian of order
2^{n-3}
. Using Lemma
3.22
, we conclude that
{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}}^{\varphi}
is
H
-conjugate to
D
. So there is some
\alpha\in\mathrm{Inn}(H)
mapping
{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}}^{\varphi}
to
D
. We may assume that
\alpha
centralizes
{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}}^{\varphi}
. Then
{H_{1}}^{\alpha}=H_{1}
, and the isomorphism
\psi:=\varphi\alpha
maps
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
to
D
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
to
H_{1}
, as desired.

Using Lemma
3.38
, we can find elements
x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell}\in N_{H}(D)
such that
H=\langle H_{1}
,
{H_{1}}^{x_{1}}
, …,
{H_{1}}^{x_{\ell}}\rangle
. Therefore,
K
has elements
m_{1}
,
\dots
,
m_{\ell}
such that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}=\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A},{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}}^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}},\dots,{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}}^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}}\rangle

and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m},\dots,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}\in N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
. From Lemma
2.1
, we see that
N_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}_{1})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{N}
. So we may assume
m_{i}\in N_{K}(E_{1})
for
i\in\{1,\dots,\ell\}
. Let
E_{0}:=\langle u,t_{\{3,4\}},t_{\{4,5\}}\rangle
. By Lemma
6.5
, we have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\trianglelefteq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
. In particular,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
normalizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
. Moreover,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{T}
. We have
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\cong SL_{2}(q^{*})
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{T}\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A})
(see Lemma
6.4
). Applying Lemma
3.43
, we conclude that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
. As
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\trianglelefteq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\leq C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})
, we even have that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{A}
is a component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
. It follows that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}=\langle L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}))^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}},\dots,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}))^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}}\rangle.

Let
k\in K
such that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{k}\in C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
. As
K\trianglelefteq C
, we have
[k,E_{0}]\leq O(C)\cap K=O(K)
. Thus
kO(K)\in C_{C/O(K)}(E_{0}O(K)/O(K))
. By Lemma
2.1
, there is an element
z\in C_{C}(E_{0})
such that
kO(K)=zO(K)
. Observing that
z\in C_{K}(E_{0})
and that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{k}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{z}
, we may conclude that
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
. If
1\leq i\leq\ell
, then
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}))^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}}=L_{2^{\prime}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}}={\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}}^{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{m}}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
, where the second equality follows from Proposition
2.4
. It follows that

K=\langle O(K),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0})),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))^{m_{1}},\dots,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))^{m_{\ell}}\rangle.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

Suppose that
n=6
,
q\equiv 3\ \mathrm{mod}\ 4
and
q^{*}\neq 3
. Let
E
denote the subgroup of
S
consisting of all
t_{A}
, where
A
is a subset of
\{1,\dots,n\}
of even order. Then
\Delta_{G}(F)=1
for any Klein four subgroup
F
of
E
.

Proof.

We follow the pattern of the proof of
[
32
, Theorem 9.1]
.

Set
W_{0}:=\langle\Delta_{G}(F)\ |\ F\leq E,m(F)=2\rangle
and
M:=N_{G}(W_{0})
. Since
T
is the image of

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&B\end{pmatrix}\ :\ A\in W,B\in V,\mathrm{det}(A)\mathrm{det}(B)=1\right\}

in
PSL_{n}(q)
, we have
T\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(PSL_{n}(q))
by Lemma
3.15
. Hence
S=T
and thus
t\in Z(S)
. By choice of
W
(see Section
5
), we have

W=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&B\\
\end{pmatrix}\ :\ A,B\in V\right\}\cdot\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}&I_{2}\\
I_{2}&\\
\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle

We accomplish the proof step by step.

(1)
For each subgroup
E_{0}
of
E
with order at least
8
, we have
N_{G}(E_{0})\leq M
.

Clearly,
E\cong E_{16}
. Therefore, the statement follows from the
2
-balance of
G
(see Lemma
7.12
) and Proposition
2.8
(ii).

(2)
N_{G}(S)\leq M
.

First we prove
S\leq M
. By (1), we have
E\leq M
. As
q\equiv 3\ \mathrm{mod}\ 4
and
S=T
, any element of
S
can be written as a product of an element of
E
and an element of
S
induced by a matrix of the form

\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&B\end{pmatrix}

with
A\in W\cap SL_{4}(q)
and
B\in V\cap SL_{2}(q)
. So, in order to prove that
S\leq M
, it suffices to show that each element of
S
induced by a matrix of this form lies in
M
. If
B\in V\cap SL_{2}(q)
, then the image of

\begin{pmatrix}I_{4}&\\
&B\end{pmatrix}

in
S
centralizes the group
\langle t_{\{1,2\}},t_{\{2,3\}},t_{\{3,4\}}\rangle\cong E_{8}
. So it is contained in
M
by (1). Hence, in order to prove that
S\leq M
, it suffices to show that if
A\in W\cap SL_{4}(q)
, then the image of

\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

in
S
lies in
M
. So assume that
A\in W\cap SL_{4}(q)
. By the structure of
W
, there are elements
M_{1}
,
M_{2}
of
V
such that
\mathrm{det}(M_{1})=\mathrm{det}(M_{2})
and

A=\begin{pmatrix}M_{1}&\\
&M_{2}\end{pmatrix}\ \textnormal{or}\ A=\begin{pmatrix}M_{1}&\\
&M_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&I_{2}\\
I_{2}&\end{pmatrix}.

The image of

\begin{pmatrix}M_{1}&&\\
&M_{2}&\\
&&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

in
S
can be written as a product of an element of
E
and an element of
S
induced by a matrix of the form

\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{M_{1}}&&\\
&\widetilde{M_{2}}&\\
&&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

with
\widetilde{M_{1}},\widetilde{M_{2}}\in V\cap SL_{2}(q)
. The images of

\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{M_{1}}&\\
&I_{4}\end{pmatrix}\ \textnormal{and}\ \begin{pmatrix}I_{2}&&\\
&\widetilde{M_{2}}&\\
&&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

in
S
centralize the groups
\langle t_{\{3,4\}},t_{\{4,5\}},t_{\{5,6\}}\rangle
and
\langle t_{\{1,2\}},t_{\{2,5\}},t_{\{5,6\}}\rangle
, respectively. So they are elements of
M
. It follows that the image of

\begin{pmatrix}M_{1}&&\\
&M_{2}&\\
&&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

in
S
lies in
M
. The image of the block matrix

\begin{pmatrix}&I_{2}&\\
I_{2}&&\\
&&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

in
S
normalizes
E
and is thus contained in
M
. It follows that the image of

\begin{pmatrix}A&\\
&I_{2}\end{pmatrix}

in
S
lies in
M
. Consequently,
S\leq M
.

By Lemma
3.23
,
\mathrm{Aut}_{PSL_{n}(q)}(S)=\mathrm{Inn}(S)
. As
\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)=\mathcal{F}_{S}(PSL_{n}(q))
, it follows that
\mathrm{Aut}_{G}(S)=\mathrm{Inn}(S)
, and so
N_{G}(S)=SC_{G}(S)
. We have seen above that
S\leq M
, and we have
C_{G}(S)\leq M
by (1). Hence
N_{G}(S)\leq M
.

(3)
C_{G}(t)\leq M
.

Let
E_{1}
be the subgroup of
X_{1}
consisting of all
t_{A}
, where
A
is a subset of
\{1,\dots,n-2\}
of even order. As a consequence of Lemma
7.14
, there is an
E_{8}
-subgroup
E_{0}
of
E
such that
K=\langle O(K),C_{K}(E_{0}),N_{K}(E_{1})\rangle
. By (1),
C_{K}(E_{0})
and
N_{K}(E_{1})
are subgroups of
M
. By
[
28
, Proposition 11.23]
, we have

O(K)=\langle C_{O(K)}(B)\ |\ B\leq E,m(B)=3\rangle.

Therefore,
O(K)\leq M
by (1). Consequently,
K\leq M
. By the Frattini argument,

C_{G}(t)=KN_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1}).

So it suffices to show that
N_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})\leq M
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)=\mathcal{F}_{S}(PSL_{n}(q))
, we may conclude from Lemma
5.7
that
\mathrm{Aut}_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})
is a
2
-group. Hence,
N_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})/C_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})
is a
2
-group. As
X_{1}\trianglelefteq T=S\in\mathrm{Syl}_{2}(C_{G}(t))
, it follows that
N_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})=SC_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})
. We have
S\leq M
by (2), and
C_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})\leq C_{G}(E_{1})\leq M
by (1). Consequently,
N_{C_{G}(t)}(X_{1})\leq M
, as required.

(4)
Let
x
be an involution of
S
which is
G
-conjugate to
t
. Then
x
is
M
-conjugate to
t
.

It is easy to see that if an element of
T
is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
t
, then it is
C_{PSL_{n}(q)}(t)
-conjugate to an element of
E
. As
\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)=\mathcal{F}_{S}(PSL_{n}(q))
and
S=T
, it follows that
x
is
C_{G}(t)
-conjugate and hence
M
-conjugate to an element
y
of
E
. It is rather easy to show that if an element of
E
is
PSL_{n}(q)
-conjugate to
t
, then it is
N_{PSL_{n}(q)}(E)
-conjugate to
t
. So, as
\mathcal{F}_{S}(G)=\mathcal{F}_{S}(PSL_{n}(q))
, we have that
y
is
N_{G}(E)
-conjugate to
t
. By (1),
N_{G}(E)\leq M
, and so
x
is
M
-conjugate to
t
.

(5)
Let
x
be an involution of
S
. Then
C_{G}(x)\leq M
.

Let
R
be a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
C_{G}(x)
with
C_{S}(x)\leq R
. We have
t\in Z(S)\leq C_{S}(x)
and
t\in M
. Thus
t\in R\cap M
. Let
r\in N_{R}(R\cap M)
. Then
y:=t^{r}\in R\cap M
. As a consequence of (4),
y
is
M
-conjugate to
t
. So there is an element
m
of
M
such that
t^{r}=y=t^{m}
. We have
rm^{-1}\in C_{G}(t)\leq M
by (3), and so
r\in R\cap M
. Hence,
N_{R}(R\cap M)=R\cap M
, and thus
R=R\cap M
.

By Corollary
7.3
,
C_{G}(x)
is
3
-generated. Therefore,
C_{G}(x)
is generated by the normalizers
N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)
, where
U\leq R
and
m(U)\geq 3
. It suffices to show that each such normalizer lies in
M
.

So let
U\leq R
with
m(U)\geq 3
, and let
g\in N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)
. Take an elementary abelian subgroup
Q
of
U
of rank
3
. Lemma
2.3
shows that any
E_{8}
-subgroup of
S
has an involution which is the image of an involution of
SL_{n}(q)
. This implies that
Q
has an element
s
which is
G
-conjugate to
t
. Since
s,s^{g}\in U\leq R\leq M
, we see from (4) that
s
and
s^{g}
are
M
-conjugate to
t
. So there are elements
m,m^{\prime}\in M
such that
s=t^{m}
and
s^{g}=t^{m^{\prime}}
. We have
t^{m^{\prime}}=s^{g}=(t^{m})^{g}=t^{mg}
. Thus
mgm^{\prime-1}\in C_{G}(t)\leq M
, and hence
g\in M
. It follows that
N_{C_{G}(x)}(U)\leq M
.

(6)
M=G
.

Assume that
M\neq G
. By
[
28
, Proposition 17.11]
, we may deduce from (2) and (5) that
M
is strongly embedded in
G
, i.e.
M\cap M^{g}
has odd order for any
g\in G\setminus M
. Applying
[
49
, Chapter 6, 4.4]
, it follows that
G
has only one conjugacy class of involutions. On the other hand, we see from Proposition
3.5
that
G
has precisely two conjugacy classes of involutions. This contradiction shows that
M=G
.

(7)
Conclusion.

Let
F
be a Klein four subgroup of
E
. Clearly,
\Delta_{G}(F)\leq W_{0}
. By (6), we have
W_{0}\trianglelefteq G
. Since
G
is
2
-balanced,
W_{0}
has odd order by Proposition
2.8
(ii). As
O(G)=1
by Hypothesis
5.1
, it follows that
W_{0}=1
. Hence
\Delta_{G}(F)=1
.
∎

7.5.
Quasisimplicity of the
2
-components of
C_{G}(t)

In this subsection, we determine the isomorphism types of
K
and
L
.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
x
and
y
be two commuting involutions of
G
. Set
C:=C_{G}(x)
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C/O(C)
. Then any
2
-component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})
is a component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})
.

Proof.

By
[
32
, Corollary 3.2]
,
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))=L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
. We know from Section
6
that
L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})
is a
K
-group, i.e. the composition factors of
L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})
are known finite simple groups. Applying
[
26
, Theorem 3.5]
, we conclude that
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))=L(C_{L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}))
. Therefore, any
2
-component of
C_{L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})
is a component of
C_{L(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C})}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})
. So any
2
-component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})
is a component of
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y})
.

Instead of using
[
26
, Theorem 3.5]
, the lemma could be proved directly by using Corollary
3.46
(i) and the results of Section
6
.
∎

Proposition 7.17
.

K
is isomorphic to a quotient of
SL_{n-2}^{\varepsilon}(q^{*})
by a central subgroup of odd order.

Proof.

The proof is inspired from the proof of
[
32
, Theorem 10.1]
.

For
q^{*}=3
, the proposition follows from Proposition
7.7
. From now on, we assume that
q^{*}\neq 3
.

Set
C:=C_{G}(t)
. Let
E
denote the subgroup of
T
consisting of all
t_{A}
, where
A\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
has even order. We assume
m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell}
, where
\ell:=n-4
, to be elements of
K
and
E_{0}
to be an
E_{8}
-subgroup of
E
with

K=\langle O(K),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0})),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))^{m_{1}},\dots,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))^{m_{\ell}}\rangle.

Such elements
m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell}
and such a subgroup
E_{0}
exist by Lemma
7.14
.

The proof will be accomplished step by step.

(1)
Let
f
be an involution of
E_{0}
. Then
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))\leq L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C}(f))
.

As
K\trianglelefteq C
, we have
C_{K}(E_{0})\trianglelefteq C_{C}(E_{0})
. This implies
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))\leq L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C}(E_{0}))
. By
[
32
, Theorem 3.1]
, we have
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C_{C}(f)}(E_{0}))\leq L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C}(f))
. Clearly,
C_{C_{C}(f)}(E_{0})=C_{C}(E_{0})
. It follows that
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))\leq L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C}(E_{0}))\leq L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C}(f))
.

(2)
Let
F
be a Klein four subgroup of
E_{0}
. Set
D:=[C_{O(K)}(F),L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))]
. Then
D=1
.

Clearly,
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))
normalizes
C_{O(K)}(F)
. Also,
O^{2^{\prime}}(L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0})))=L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))
, and
C_{O(K)}(F)
is a
2^{\prime}
-group. Applying
[
28
, Proposition 4.3 (i)]
, we conclude that
D=[D,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))]
.

Now let
f
be an involution of
F
. We are going to show that
D\leq O(C_{G}(f))
. Set
M:=L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{C}(f))
. By (1),
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))\leq M
. Also,
D\leq C_{C}(F)\leq C_{C}(f)
and
M\trianglelefteq C_{C}(f)
. It follows that
D=[D,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))]\leq[C_{C}(f),M]\leq M
.

Let
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}:=C_{G}(f)/O(C_{G}(f))
. By Corollary
2.2
,
C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}
. As a consequence of Proposition
2.4
,
L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t}))=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
. Lemma
7.16
implies that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}=L(C_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{C}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t}))
. It easily follows that
O(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})
is central in
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
.

From the definition of
D
, it is clear that
D\leq O(K)
. So we have
D\leq M\cap O(K)\leq O(M)
. It follows that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}\leq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{O}\leq O(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})\leq Z(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})
. In particular,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}
centralizes
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{D}
. Thus
D=[D,L_{2^{\prime}}(C_{K}(E_{0}))]\leq O(C_{G}(f))
.

Since
f
